I just haven't had any real interest in doing them.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, this low spell has been hanging on for... I'd say about three months now. I mean, after that burst of energy I had wore off, it just felt like somebody had completely pulled the plug on me. Most of the time now, I just... I'll sit at the kitchen table staring at my coffee. I just feel completely empty. And ashamed, honestly. I mean, I'm tired, sure, but I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than normal. And I definitely haven't been restless or pacing around or anything. I just sit there, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or so good that people around you noticed a significant change in your mood or behavior?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. But it wasn't just a good feeling, I was also pretty on edge. I remember snapping at people over little things down at the coffee shop. I just felt like I knew better than everyone else.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic and overly confident, was there a clear period where your mood was noticeably elevated or irritable — like you were feeling unusually good or agitated — that was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it was definitely different from my usual self. It was just day after day of feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic, overly confident, and irritable, did you need less sleep than usual, such as feeling rested after only a few hours of sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely didn't need much sleep back then. I just couldn't really sit still. I was bouncing from filling out job applications to... well, sketching out these woodworking projects, and then I'd jump right over to rewriting my resume.
